# PITFALLS — global index (grep before you code)

The tag-first index into every lesson. Each line LEADS with its full bracketed
tag set, so retrieval is `grep` by tag regardless of grouping:

    grep '\[prosemirror\]' PITFALLS.md
    grep '\[ssml\]'        PITFALLS.md

Line format: `` `[tag][tag]` one-line lesson → projects/<file>.md#<id> ``
Group headers below are a human-scanning convenience only; the tags are the index.

After a real fix, add ONE line here and the full entry in the project file.
Append-only — correct a stale line with a new one, don't rewrite history.

## Tags in use (reuse before inventing)
- domains: `[browser]` `[editor]` `[process]` `[tts]` `[ssml]` `[gamedev]` `[coop]`
  `[phaser]` `[gamedesign]` `[input]` `[stats]`
- modifiers: `[tampermonkey]` `[events]` `[radix]` `[popover]` `[prosemirror]`
  `[observability]` `[voice]` `[prompt-engineering]` `[elevenlabs]` `[text-processing]`
  `[camera]` `[state]` `[physics]` `[vite]` `[tooling]` `[harness]` `[git]`

---

## browser
- `[browser][tampermonkey][events]` Synthetic pointer/mouse events under a Tampermonkey `@grant`
  sandbox: `view: window` is rejected ("Failed to convert value to 'Window'"). Use the real Window
  (`unsafeWindow` / `document.defaultView`), with a fallback that omits `view`.
  → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E1
- `[browser][radix][popover]` Detect popovers/menus by a visibility transition, not by DOM insertion —
  many frameworks pre-render them hidden and toggle visible.
  → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E2

## editor
- `[editor][prosemirror]` A "select-all + delete" clear may leave default nodes; don't assume an empty
  editor. Align by trailing position and verify node counts.
  → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E3

## process
- `[process][observability]` Instrument the lowest-level primitive first (does the click even
  dispatch?). A captured error beats a clever guess; silent failure sends you chasing the wrong layer.
  → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E4
- `[process][harness][git]` If a push to an external remote is refused no matter the auth, it's the
  harness/environment blocking it — hand the user a script to push locally instead of retrying.
  → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E10

## audio
- `[tts][voice][prompt-engineering]` If a styling directive silently stops applying mid-document, the
  renderer likely resets state at block boundaries — re-declare it per block, and close bits with an
  explicit exit tag. → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E6
- `[tts][voice][prompt-engineering]` A TTS engine may accept-but-ignore some bracket tags (silent
  no-op, e.g. `[building]`); keep an explicit dead-tags deny-list — silent no-ops never surface
  themselves. → projects/pappydapimp69__pocketzane.md#E5
- `[ssml][elevenlabs][text-processing]` `<br/>` is a silent no-op in SSML; `<break>` is the only pause
  primitive — grep for `<br` before shipping. → projects/local__warden-fables.md#E1

## gamedev
- `[gamedev][coop][camera]` Spawn co-op players together — a midpoint camera frames nothing if they
  start apart. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E1
- `[coop][state]` Clear a relationship at the point you act on it, not only at the event that ends one
  side. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E2
- `[stats][process]` A tracked stat needs a writer at its event, not just a reader at its display.
  → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E3
- `[input][coop]` If two systems read the same input edge, one fires when you don't want it — gate by
  context or give it its own button. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E4
- `[phaser][physics]` Deactivate a one-shot pickup the instant it's taken — don't let a despawn
  animation gate further hits. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E5
- `[phaser][physics][process]` Cap max frame delta or any stall becomes missed collisions.
  → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E6
- `[phaser][vite][tooling]` With HMR, anything owning a global resource (canvas/loop/socket) needs an
  explicit dispose hook. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E7
- `[phaser][physics]` Never trust positional args in an overlap callback — resolve each participant by
  type. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E8
- `[gamedesign]` A multiplier meant to help should be clamped so it can't become a penalty at the other
  end of the range. → projects/pappydapimp__lockstep.md#E9
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][tooling]` Making content data-driven opts you out of compile-time safety — a typo'd id ships an uncompletable quest with no error; add a validation ladder (schema → referential-integrity/completability → headless smoke playthrough) in the same change or breakage moves from compiler to player. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E1
- `[gamedev][state][coop]` Store the seeded RNG's `{seed,count}` inside the saved world and ban ambient `Math.random()`/`Date.now()` in the sim — one discipline gives exact save/load, replay, and the lockstep-co-op option; physics float-nondeterminism must stay out of the authoritative layer. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E2
- `[input][gamedev][harness]` Gamepad on web: poll `navigator.getGamepads()` every frame (don't trust `gamepadconnected` — Chrome hides the pad until a button press), make menus navigable by BOTH stick (debounced) and D-pad, and label on-screen buttons with words not glyphs or players press blind. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E3
- `[gamedev][state][gamedesign]` Run real-time combat over a turn-based authoritative reducer by making the "retaliation" an opt-out flag (default keeps old behavior + the golden replay test) plus a separate ENEMY_STRIKE command; implement dodge i-frames by withholding the damage command, not by adding an `invulnerable` flag to saved sim state. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E4
- `[gamedev][state][camera]` A "read-only" renderer over authoritative sim state is one stray `=` away from silently mutating it (`p.y = ...` inside camera code threw nothing, would corrupt the save) — enforce the boundary mechanically (freeze the state / lint assignments to `world.*` outside the sim), don't rely on vigilance. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E5
- `[gamedev][state][process]` A modal overlay reused for multiple roles (start / how-to / victory) leaks state: it showed start-screen class-select mid-game (accidental restart) and its close path left `paused` set (controls dead until pause→Resume). Tag the mode explicitly, gate content on it, and funnel every dismissal through one handler that restores ALL global flags. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E6
- `[gamedev][tooling][vite]` Hand-vendoring a split ESM lib (Three's `three.module.js` re-imports `./three.core.js`) needs the whole sibling set or it 404s blank; and headless WebGL is null by default — pass `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader` AND degrade gracefully, or 3D "it loaded" tests are false-green. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E7
- `[audio][process][browser]` Web Audio rhythm must be scheduled on the AudioContext clock via a look-ahead window (short timer tops up a ~150ms horizon at absolute currentTime), never per-note setTimeout — JS timers drift and are throttled when backgrounded; add a Screen Wake Lock for playback that must survive a locked phone. → projects/pappydapimp69__shadow.md#E1
- `[audio][browser][process]` Never `await` AudioContext resume/start on a critical path — Brave's privacy shields leave `resume()` pending forever, so gating game start on it locks those users out (movement still works, hiding the cause); init audio fire-and-forget and guard every call. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E1
- `[process][harness]` A benchmark must exercise the algorithm's real cost driver, not a proxy — diffing "random" strings over a tiny alphabet showed Myers as fast (small alphabet forces high overlap → low edit-distance D), hiding its O((N+M)·D) quadratic worst case; use unique/low-overlap inputs to drive D. → projects/local__sandbox-myers-diff.md#E1
- `[tooling][process]` Parser/DSL errors need WHERE, not just WHAT — carry each token's source column from the lexer and thread it into every throw ("expected ) at col 8" + a caret), decided up front, because retrofitting positions means touching every error site. → projects/local__sandbox-expr.md#E1
- `[harness][process]` A comparative test ("heuristic beats baseline") gives a false tie unless inputs are adversarial to the BASELINE — a lucky input ordering let naive scheduling pick well too (tie), and only failed (4 vs 3) once independent tasks were ordered first; construct the baseline's worst case. → projects/local__sandbox-toposort.md#E1
- `[tooling][process][harness]` ESM has no implicit main check — top-level test/CLI code (esp. `process.exit`) in an importable module RUNS on import and can kill the importer (its tests silently never run); gate it behind `import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href`. → projects/local__sandbox-myers-diff.md#E2
- `[harness][gamedev][process]` Headless rAF runs ~0.4× wall-clock (swiftshader, no vsync) so in-game time lags real time — assert on the game's own clock/state (poll it, or advance via a hook), never on real elapsed seconds, or timing tests flake. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E2
- `[state][gamedev][process]` One NaN silently poisons everything downstream of shared state (a `.z` written on a Vector2 → undefined → NaN corrupted dog→camera→audio-panners→AI, no error thrown); use correct vector types and `isFinite`-guard the boundaries where shared state fans out. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E3
- `[audio][browser][process]` No reliable web API for output geometry: `maxChannelCount` reports 2 even for mono BT, `enumerateDevices` is mic-permission-gated/empty on iOS, and transport type is unexposed — gate correctness on a one-time user-confirmed functional test ("did the sound move L→R?"), use the APIs for display only. → projects/pappydapimp69__shadow.md#E2
- `[process][tooling]` A greedy `[0-9.]+` number lexer + `parseFloat` silently truncates malformed multi-dot numbers (`"1.2.3"→1.2`, no error) — a silent-wrong result; validate the lexeme or use `Number()` (NaN on garbage) not `parseFloat` (salvages a prefix), so bad input fails loudly. → projects/local__sandbox-expr.md#E2
- `[harness][process]` When an algorithm's output isn't unique (diff scripts, minimal paths, tokenizations), don't golden-assert the exact output — property-test the invariant: reconstruct both inputs from a diff's op list (keep+ins→B, keep+del→A) and check the cost is minimal. → projects/local__sandbox-myers-diff.md#E3
- `[process][harness]` Kahn's topo-sort "unschedulable leftover" detects a dependency cycle but over-reports it (includes nodes merely downstream of the cycle) — to name the ACTUAL cycle in an error, use a DFS that catches a back-edge to a gray (on-stack) node and slice the path off the recursion stack. → projects/local__sandbox-toposort.md#E2
- `[gamedev][process][state]` A single rAF loop ticking many subsystems shares fate — one subsystem's unhandled exception blanks the whole frame; wrap each subsystem call in a try/catch (`safe()`) so faults are contained and the rest of the frame still renders. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E4
- `[browser][tooling][process]` Build-step-free static ES modules: a "logic bug" (button never updates) was stale module caching serving old code — cache-bust imports with a per-deploy token (`?v=<commit-sha>`) and render a visible build id so cache state is observable, not guessed. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E5
- `[input][browser][touch]` Mobile interactive tiles/rows over a scrollable area eat scroll drags unless given `touch-action: pan-y` (hand vertical panning back to the browser) — the inverse of a game canvas that wants `touch-action: none` to own every gesture; choose per element. → projects/pappydapimp69__shadow.md#E3
- `[audio][process]` Frequently-triggered Web Audio SFX (a mashable bark building 15–30 nodes each) degrades playback with no bounds — add a cooldown + a concurrent-voice budget (~16) + `onended` disconnect of the whole node chain; connected/scheduled nodes aren't freed for you. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E6
- `[gamedev][state][process]` When a source-of-truth object is replaced at runtime (world reload, archetype switch), rebuild ALL derived/mirrored state (meshes, caches) through ONE shared clear+respawn path — per-call-site partial cleanup duplicated villagers (leaked the collection it forgot) and froze enemies bound to stale entity ids. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E8
- `[audio][state]` A "between-repeats" delay applied after the final step keeps a one-shot in a transient playing state for a gap that never precedes anything — zero the tail delay when repeat is off; suspect it whenever one code path serves both looping and one-shot modes. → projects/pappydapimp69__shadow.md#E4
- `[process][git][ci]` A branch-protection rule is a claim, not a fact — never trust an access-control rule you haven't watched refuse something; test the gate with the actor it's meant to stop (apps/bypass lists can make it a silent no-op). → projects/pappydapimp69__memory.md#E1
- `[gamedev][harness][process]` Headless browser testing: give each concurrent Playwright/http.server run its OWN port (a shared port causes phantom "logic" failures that pass on re-run), and gesture-initialize WebAudio (create/resume AudioContext in a tap/click handler) or audio is silently dead under autoplay policy. → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E9
- `[narrative][structure]` events-in-a-row plus polish is a chronicle; a story needs want/opposition/stakes/choice — audit structure before pacing or voice → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E10
- `[animation][sync]` data-sized text and authored motion drift under any scale factor — pin them at semantic anchors and piecewise-warp between pins → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E11
- `[tts][voice]` sync visuals to the TTS engine's real speaking state, not an estimated reading time — estimates drift per device/voice and cut the voice off → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E12
- `[process][harness]` reused draw helpers silently diverge on shared constant names; verify a time-based renderer with a full seek-sweep, not a few sample frames → projects/pappydapimp69__test.md#E13
- `[process][tooling][cross-platform]` A "cross-platform" Python CLI usually hides three Unix assumptions — symlinks (need admin on Windows → use a .cmd shim), the `python3` name (often just `python`), and unicode console output (cp1252 crashes on `✓`/emoji → detect + ASCII-fallback); test on a real Windows console early. → projects/pappydapimp69__brain.md#E1
- `[gamedev][physics][state]` An agent with momentum that seeks a target at constant full speed overshoots and orbits it forever — give it an arrival/slowing radius that ramps desired speed to zero so it settles (naive seek crosses the target endlessly; arrival crosses once). → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E7
- `[gamedev][physics][state]` In a sim where agents react to each other (boids/N-body/crowd), read neighbour state from a snapshot taken before anyone moves, then integrate — updating positions in place mid-loop makes the tick iteration-order-dependent and breaks force symmetry (forward-vs-reversed regression catches it). → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E8
- `[browser][state][process]` A `safe()` frame-loop wrapper never catches module *load-time* throws: calling a helper that reads a `let` declared further down hits the TDZ and silently kills the whole module — keep init dependency-ordered. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E9
- `[gamedev][physics][state]` Per-frame smoothing/lerp-follow must use `1 - pow(base, dt)` (not a constant per-frame fraction) or it's frame-rate-dependent — a fixed k converges ~2× faster at 60 fps than 30 fps and jerks on frame spikes. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E10
- `[gamedev][state][process]` Give every behaviour-FSM state a guaranteed exit (max-dwell timeout / unconditional default), not only a data-driven guard — an unreachable target or never-flipped flag silently traps the agent in that state forever (test by breaking one guard and asserting all states still get visited). → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E11
- `[gamedev][state][process]` A seed only reproduces a generated world if EVERY roll goes through the one seeded stream — one leaked Math.random()/Date.now() in the generation path silently breaks it (verify: same seed twice = byte-identical, and grep the path for ambient entropy). → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E12
- `[gamedev][performance][state]` A uniform spatial-hash grid must scan the 3×3 block of cells (home + 8 neighbours), cell size ≥ radius — scanning only the home cell silently drops cross-boundary neighbours (~2/3 of them); validate pair counts against brute force. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E13
- `[browser][audio][process]` Don't drive Web Audio note timing from setTimeout — under main-thread jank it schedules notes in the past and they bunch/stutter; use a look-ahead scheduler against audioCtx.currentTime (horizon ≥ one dropped frame), and snap the clock to now when muted. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E14
- `[physics][gamedev][state]` A discrete "does my current position overlap this obstacle" collision check tunnels for any object whose per-frame travel exceeds the obstacle's thickness — deliberately test at that exact speed boundary; use swept/segment collision (or sub-stepping) for anything that can move fast (bullets, fast balls, projectiles). → projects/local__sandbox-collision.md#E1
- `[gamedesign][algorithm][state]` A "harder/better farther from spawn" gradient must use graph/BFS distance over the walkable map, never Euclidean distance, once the level has any obstacles — a cell can be straight-line-close to spawn while requiring a walk many times longer (65x in one test) to actually reach; one BFS from spawn gives the correct distance field. → projects/local__sandbox-distance.md#E1
- `[gamedesign][algorithm][state]` Placing a key/prerequisite object uniformly at random over a generated level (with a lock depending on it elsewhere) produces unsolvable levels at a real rate (~20% in one test) — always restrict placement to the reachable set computed with that lock treated as impassable; verify solvability across many seeds, not by eye. → projects/local__sandbox-keylock.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state][process]` A procedural generator needing a global guarantee (maze/dungeon connectivity, level solvability) can't get it from "roll each piece open at a density tuned to match a known-good average" — matching total openness doesn't make the arrangement connect; use a construction that guarantees the property (e.g. a spanning-tree carve) and verify with an actual flood-fill over many seeds. → projects/local__sandbox-maze.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedesign][state]` Independent uniform-random object scattering clumps measurably (overlapping/crowded pairs, uneven gaps) because draws have no memory of each other — "looks random" and "looks evenly spaced" are different properties; use min-distance rejection sampling (or Poisson-disk) when even spacing is the actual intent, and measure via nearest-neighbor distance stats, not a glance. → projects/local__sandbox-scatter.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state]` `array.sort(() => Math.random()-0.5)` is NOT a fair shuffle — it's measurably biased (a card can land in one slot 65%+ more/less often than uniform) despite looking random by eye; always use Fisher-Yates instead, and verify any "should be uniform" feature with a many-trial histogram, not a glance. → projects/local__sandbox-shuffle.md#E1
- `[gamedev][state][physics]` A velocity estimated by differencing position across frames misreads any teleport/respawn/warp as one frame of enormous real movement (spiking a predictive-pursuit chaser's aim) — explicitly reset the prev-position/velocity tracking at every teleport call site, don't rely on threshold-detecting the jump after the fact. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E15
- `[gamedev][state][process]` Independent agents can safely commit to the same shared resource without a claim/lock step — but only if every agent re-validates the resource is still available immediately before each dependent action (not just at commit time); stress-test by forcing N agents onto one resource and asserting exactly one succeeds while the rest revert cleanly. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E16
- `[algorithm][gamedev][state]` Min-distance-rejection scatter only protects against points it's told about — mixing fixed/hand-placed props with a procedural scatter in the same space needs EVERY fixed prop's position seeded into the rejection set first, or a random prop can silently overlap one; verify with a pairwise overlap check, not a glance. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E17
- `[coop][state][gamedev]` Share-code import must atomically replace full state, never piecemeal merge — two "merge" sources will silently disagree on authority → projects/pappydapimp69__chronicles.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev][state]` A world's generic procedural scatter (trees, decor) needs an explicit exclusion for every hand-authored sub-zone (districts, set-pieces) built elsewhere in the same world — a spawn-only exclusion list won't implicitly cover a themed zone added later, and it only surfaces on the unlucky seed; verify with a pairwise overlap check across many seeds. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E18
- `[algorithm][gamedev][state]` Min-distance-rejection scatter that seeds every fixed prop (E17) can still overlap one if seeded points carry different collision radii — a flat `minDist` under-rejects around anything bigger than it was tuned for; reject on `minDist + otherPoint.radius`, verified by a pairwise overlap check. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E19
- `[audio][dsp][harness]` A bounded-output nonlinearity (an FM operator is `sin(...)`, likewise `tanh`/hard-clip) pins peak amplitude by construction, so a peak/amplitude meter is structurally blind to instability inside it — measure the failure where it actually lives (aliased/inharmonic energy over a full-render FFT), and drive the parameter only within its real hardware range or over-driving swamps the effect. → projects/local__sandbox-genesis-fm.md#E1
- `[audio][dsp][state]` Don't assume a noise/PRNG LFSR is maximal-length — measure the period (steps to state-repeat); a tap mask is maximal only for a primitive polynomial and real hardware often ships a non-primitive one on purpose (Sega SN76489 white-noise tap 0x0009 → period 57337, not 65535), so a "period must be 2^n-1" assertion rejects the hardware-correct behavior; for fidelity copy the actual tap, not the textbook one. → projects/local__sandbox-genesis-psg.md#E1
- `[audio][dsp][harness]` A radix-2 FFT returns SILENT garbage on non-power-of-2 length — guard with `(n & (n-1))===0` and throw, because any upstream length-changing stage (resampling, trimming) violates it and two different inputs can then both emit identical garbage, faking a "they match"; feed a power-of-2 slice and compare on ABSOLUTE energy, not a per-buffer-normalized spectrum. → projects/local__sandbox-genesis-mix.md#E1
- `[ui][gamedesign][input]` When a player-facing action space grows past a couple of verbs, resolve "what can happen right now" through ONE function returning at most one action, and drive every UI surface (prompt text, button label, world-space indicator) off that single value — it stays consistent for free as verbs are added and gives you one seam to temporarily override the input (a QTE window, a cutscene) instead of many independent ones. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E20
- `[workflow][gamedev][testing]` When a user asks for many autonomous build cycles, convert the loop into structured product data and smoke-guard the requested counts instead of leaving progress as chat-only narration. → projects/local__dbh.md#E1
- `[gamedev][ui][loop]` Modal overlays that pause the main update loop also pause canvas minigames unless the modal path explicitly keeps their update/render tick alive. → projects/local__dbh.md#E2
- `[gamedev][backlog][testing]` Internal agent-facing roadmap items should close through docs + smoke-test evidence, not leak into the game as player-facing UI/rewards — only add game UI when the player can understand it as part of the fiction. → projects/local__dbh.md#E3
- `[gamedev][save][ux]` A "New Game" button must rebuild authoritative world state via the real constructor/reset path, not merely reopen character creation. → projects/local__dbh.md#E4
- `[gamedev][onboarding][save]` Onboard an already-built RPG with a small state overlay that observes existing gameplay events, rather than reshuffling global campaign objective indexes — map tutorial objects narrowly so they don't satisfy later quests. → projects/local__dbh.md#E5
- `[github][pages][deployment]` If GitHub Pages' dynamic branch-source run is stuck queued with no jobs, add an explicit Pages Actions workflow and verify the `github-pages` environment policy instead of waiting. → projects/local__dbh.md#E6
- `[gamedev][state][testing][determinism]` Canonicalize (sorted-key) serialization before hashing game state for golden/regression tests, save a seeded PRNG's raw state words (not a replay count) for O(1) load, and mechanically grep-guard the authoritative sim against transcendental Math — sharpens the existing replay-fingerprint and seeded-rng kernels → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E1
- `[input][gamedev][harness]` Capture input presses at event time into a pending queue, not by sampling held state once per frame — a press shorter than one frame is invisible to pure sampling (found via Playwright's instant keypresses; affects real fast taps too) → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E2
- `[input][ui][gamedev]` Refines pappydapimp69/test#E3 one layer further: a dialog/modal's key-hint text must be recomputed from the active device at RENDER time, not baked into the label when the UI element is constructed — the device active at creation time is not guaranteed to still be active at render time → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E3
- `[process][gamedev]` The same "turn this data into player-facing text" logic drifted apart TWICE across two UI surfaces (once each, independently) when a new discriminant value was added — extract shared text-generation logic into one function the FIRST time you notice duplication, not after a second bug proves it was needed → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E4
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][ux]` Two unrelated systems reacting to the same named game event (e.g. an onboarding checklist and a quest's kill count both triggered by "hit a husk") will read as ONE requirement if their player-facing text/UI sit near each other — audit adjacent text for accidentally-implied dependencies the code doesn't have, and get ground truth from a headless test before assuming a reported "logic bug" is real. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E5
- `[input][gamedev][ui]` A unified input module's one-shot "press" output must not be gated behind the fixed vocabulary used for continuously-held actions — any UI zone with a dynamic id (title screen, settings menu) needs to fire a press too; modal buttons only worked "by accident" because their ids happened to already be in that list → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E6
- `[input][gamedesign]` A hold-to-charge meter needs an authoritative hold-counter that resets on a fresh press (not on release-based inference) and a rate shaped by BOTH hold duration and current fill percent — throttle near-full hard enough to override the ramp bonus, or a long hold defeats the intended slowdown; needs the input layer to expose continuous held state alongside edge-triggered presses → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E7
- `[browser][tooling][process]` An entry-point-only `?v=` cache-bust on a static ES-module app's `<script>` tag refreshes the entry file but NOT its nested relative imports (separate cache entries) — bust every import specifier at deploy time or explicitly document the gap, or the next "still broken after deploy" report gets re-diagnosed as a phantom logic bug. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E8
- `[gamedev][quest][state]` A quest objective can't be soft-locked by pre-acceptance actions if its target entities don't exist until ACCEPT_QUEST spawns them — reject retroactive credit-on-accept (still history-dependent) in favor of making completion provably agnostic of history by construction; mirrors local__dbh#E5 in reverse direction → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E9
- `[gamedev][rendering][canvas]` A canvas helper that blits a full-screen layer by resetting the transform must save/restore it, or the caller's next world-space draws (occluder walls, here) land in raw screen space and stop tracking the camera → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E1
- `[gamedev][state][save]` Restart-on-death must restore a JSON snapshot of the run's start, not `makeWorld(seed, options)` — a Continue reaches the same restart path with options empty, silently resurrecting the player as the default archetype; also never autosave a hp-0/ended world → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E2
- `[gamedev][design][state]` A mechanic that scales with repeated attempts at one encounter is only viable if retrying is actually cheap — read the literal respawn/retry code path first; a full-session-revert respawn (snapshot the whole world at session start, restore on death) silently rules out attempt-based design for any one-shot fight → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E1
- `[gamedev][harness][testing][determinism]` A browser e2e test that drives the LIVE running game with a fixed-coordinate scripted command sequence breaks the instant any entity gains real-time autonomous movement (patrol AI, ambient traffic) — real wall-clock time before/between commands lets it wander off the assumed position; re-read entity positions live before each interaction instead of reusing a pure-replay test's static coordinates → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E2
- `[gamedev][ui][state]` A single command that can emit multiple events which each independently and unconditionally set the same view state (e.g. `'talked'` opening a shop modal, `'quests_offered'` overwriting it) makes it unsafe to give one NPC/entity two roles whose handlers both react to that command — keep roles on separate entities unless the handlers are taught to coordinate → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E3
- `[gamedev][state][gamedesign]` A "shouldn't spawn instantly" complaint about something with gameplay stakes needs a sim-side existence delay (the entity genuinely absent from state until a scheduled tick), not a renderer-only fade-in — a cosmetic ease-in on a mechanically-already-live entity decouples the visual telegraph from what the sim actually guarantees, which reads as more broken than an honest instant pop → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E4
- `[gamedev][design]` Before generalizing a mechanic to a new edge case, check whether the case actually produces variance the mechanic can respond to — if the case's trigger is a fixed event every player hits identically, extending the mechanic there is just relabeling, not rewarding; keep it on its old mechanism as a named exception instead → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E5
- `[gamedev][rendering][canvas][perf]` Canvas 2D `ctx.filter` (grayscale/hue-rotate/etc) applied individually before every `drawImage` in a per-tile/per-entity draw loop causes cost that scales with draw count, not frame count (measured: one `render()` call at 19.3s) — composite the whole scene into an offscreen buffer UNFILTERED, then apply the filter ONCE to a single blit of that buffer → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E3
- `[gamedev][quest][state]` Offering N choices as one exclusive batch requires the accept handler to explicitly withdraw the other pending offers — leaving only a comment saying they're "withdrawn" without code that does it lets a player accept more than one, and a test suite that always re-queries between picks will never catch the gap → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E4
- `[state][gamedev]` A sparse map whose values carry a magnitude that can legitimately be 0 (opacity, a modifier, a count) must be existence-checked (`in`/`hasOwnProperty`), never truthy-checked — `if (map[key])` silently treats a real 0 value as absent, and only a test case with a zero-magnitude example can catch it → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E5
- `[input][ui][gamedev]` A UI hint and the handler it describes drift silently when authored independently — for a single-interaction control, hardcode the hint to match the ONE handler (or derive it from the same source), don't trust a per-caller `hintAction` field that can name a button the handler never checks → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E6
- `[input][browser][touch][mobile]` `'ontouchstart' in window` alone false-negatives on real touch hardware (notably iPadOS Safari's default desktop-site mode) — any UI default gated on it (e.g. show on-screen touch controls by default) silently fails there; OR in `navigator.maxTouchPoints > 0`. When a bug report says a control "doesn't show by default" rather than "doesn't respond," suspect a one-time capability check, and reproduce it by faking the platform signals directly rather than needing physical hardware → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E6
- `[gamedev][rendering][mobile]` A top-down camera's world scale formula that fits N tiles to canvas HEIGHT alone is a landscape-only assumption — H is only the constraining/shorter dimension on a landscape screen; on mobile portrait H is the LARGER raw-pixel dimension and the same formula over-zooms, cropping most of the width. Use `Math.min(W, H)` to fit the actually-shorter axis, and verify with a real portrait-viewport screenshot, not just a resized desktop window → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E7
- `[gamedev][tooling][process]` A gap already catalogued (especially one marked "deferred"/"partial") in one project can silently exist, un-fixed, in every SIBLING project that copied the same boilerplate before the gap was known — a bug report that doesn't reproduce against current source should be checked against the project's own memory catalog for this shape before being treated as a fresh investigation, and the fix applied to every sibling sharing the pattern, not just the one reported → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E8
- `[browser][mobile][harness]` `100vh/100vw` sizes to the LARGEST (chrome-collapsed) mobile viewport, so bottom-anchored UI falls below the fold when the address bar shows — use `100dvh/100dvw` with `vh/vw` as a preceding fallback. Corollary: a headless browser has no collapsible chrome, so it CANNOT reproduce this class of bug — "headless-green but broken on device" is a category signal that the trigger is a mobile-chrome/viewport condition headless doesn't model → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E7
- `[ui][event-ordering][gamedev]` One user action emitting multiple sequential presentation events (`reduce -> events[] -> for(e of events) onEvent(e)`) that each independently set the SAME UI slot (a modal, toast, focus target) is a last-write-wins race — a later event's handler can silently discard an earlier one's UI effect. A live QA check that only inspects one event's effect in isolation (a raw state read right after that one event) can pass while the real multi-event UI, verified via an actual screenshot of the full action's outcome, never shows it. Make the later handler compose with the earlier one's content instead of unconditionally overwriting it. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E1
- `[save][state][gamedev]` A "restore on failure" checkpoint conflating WHAT progress to keep with WHERE to place the player tends to get exactly one right by accident — refresh progress on the same gate as the real save, but resolve position/status independently (a fixed safe spot, not the literal failure point) or a fast-refreshing checkpoint can drop the player right back into the thing that just killed them; also audit for closure/external state (e.g. a cooldown map) that a "restore" doesn't touch. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E2
- `[determinism][testing][gamedev]` A multi-tick test asserting on a probabilistic sim outcome (spread/AI rolls) is implicitly coupled to every other RNG-consuming entity in the world — an unrelated content change (e.g. gating a free-roam enemy) can shift the shared RNG stream and flip a previously-passing test; isolate the mechanic under test (block spread paths, etc.), don't just tune coordinates against today's stream. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E3
- `[rendering][gamedev][content]` A graceful fallback for a missing kind-keyed sprite/asset map entry (never crash, draw a placeholder) also means a forgotten entry produces zero build-time signal — add a smoke test asserting every content-side kind id resolves in the presentation-side map, the same way referential-integrity validation already covers sim-side ids, or the exact bug recurs on every new kind added after the first fix. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E4
- `[gamedev][state]` A reducer case that mutates a NEW nested state field (`state.a.b += 1`) added in the same pass as the constructor is one missing initializer away from silently poisoning that field with NaN forever (`undefined + 1 = NaN`, and `NaN += 1` stays NaN) — no error is ever thrown, only the resulting gate/comparison quietly always fails. Grep the constructor for the exact field name right after writing the reducer case that touches it. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E1
- `[gamedev][state][gamedesign]` A reducer gate that reads its own threshold from the gated COMMAND's payload (`cmd.threshold || default`) is bypassable by simply omitting the field — move the threshold into state set once before the gated command can influence it (e.g. a constructor option), never re-supply it per-dispatch. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E2
- `[gamedev][state][process]` Put autonomous-agent AI INSIDE the replayed authoritative reducer, not the render layer, or your golden/determinism test silently exempts your hardest subsystem — pay the tax (seed from the sim RNG, no wall-clock/float, constant RNG-roll count per decision so later content can't re-perturb same-tick movers). → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E9
- `[gamedesign][state][gamedev]` When one world property (light/noise/heat) could drive several mechanics, split it into a DISCRETE event signal vs a CONTINUOUS field and wire each mechanic to the one it wants — here detection = a transient ping pulse, comfort = the standing-light field, so standing light never reveals; simpler "bright = spotted" is given up for a legible, action-caused stealth rule you must then teach. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E5
- `[gamedev][state][gamedesign]` Draw the sim/presentation boundary by CONSEQUENCE: a telegraph/arming delay that gates real damage goes in authoritative fingerprinted state (armed bottle + `armTicks` countdown, discrete detonation event) so it replays bit-exact — paying a WORLD_VERSION bump and bigger saves — while only the cosmetic whiteout stays renderer-side; a wall-clock delay in the view layer that gates damage is a determinism hole. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E6
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][state]` Model enemy behaviour variants as a per-instance `role` tag on one shared kind (branch in the shared decision fn) instead of minting a new kind per variant — you reuse all art/stats/AI for free, but the variants go invisible to the kind registry and lose per-type validation, so add an allow-list/smoke check for the tags. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E7
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][rendering]` To show a continuous 0-100 field in a nearest-neighbor pixel-art renderer, an ordered Bayer dither was chosen over a smooth alpha gradient: it looks native to the blocky grain and needs no per-pixel alpha (the sprites are baked opaque anyway), at the cost of quantized 6-step falloff and a darkest bucket capped short of true black. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E8
- `[gamedesign][state][gamedev]` Teach a signature verb by withholding the world until it's used (gate the ambient sight radius on a one-way `hasPinged` flag → black screen pre-ping) instead of a tutorial string — durable, and doubles as fiction, but trades guaranteed comprehension for discovery and only works with an always-visible control legend to catch the player. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E9
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][state]` Compute progress-tracking NPC/quest dialog from live quest state via a priority ladder (most-progressed-first, reusing the HUD/objective ordering you already trust) instead of static text — you gain lines that always match the player's stage, but the branch ids become unchecked references, so smoke-test the ladder or a renamed quest silently strands a line. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E10
- `[gamedev][rendering]` Do a walk-behind/occlusion fade with `globalAlpha` on the existing fill primitives on the live canvas, not `ctx.filter`/offscreen compositing in a per-tile hot loop (a known frame-time trap a sibling game already hit) — cheap and scales, at the cost of flat-alpha's ceiling (no grayscale/blur); only pay for compositing when the effect truly needs per-pixel color work. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E10
- `[gamedev][state][tooling]` In a data-driven game, deliberately choose whether a save SNAPSHOTS content (deep-copy definitions into state at construction) or REFERENCES it (ids + live lookup): snapshotting gives save stability + deterministic replay — a save never shifts under a content edit — but the same property means a save can never RECEIVE a content hotfix; pair snapshotting with additive-only schema versioning and a version stamp that fails safe to New Game. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E10
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][quest]` A genuine tension Brain surfaces between two prior projects' precedents (e.g. effect-gated-always-present vs existence-gated-on-demand interactables) may not be a true disagreement on mechanism — check whether each precedent was actually correct for a DIFFERENT CLASS of object (discovery-reward objects vs system-guarantee-protecting objects); if so, resolve it with a classification rule per object kind, not a single project-wide pick. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E3
- `[process][tooling][git]` For static-hosting deploys, prefer an explicit inspectable CI workflow with your test suite as its first gate over a zero-config "dynamic"/auto Pages builder — the auto builder saves a YAML file but its failure mode is a silent queue-with-no-jobs that reports success while deploying nothing (far costlier to diagnose than the workflow is to maintain); wire smoke/tests in as the gate so deploy doubles as a release check. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E11
- `[gamedev][gamedesign]` For a sequel's signature system, prefer growing an EXISTING stat a tier (perception → AI-legibility) over inventing a new reveal mechanic — buys series identity and zero relearning, costs novelty; gate the new tier on per-kind EXPOSURE (win AND loss) not a stat that rises without meeting the enemy, and route the added save field through a clean WORLD_VERSION bump. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E11
- `[gamedev][state][testing][determinism]` When adding new authoritative state on top of an existing golden replay-fingerprint, prefer a SEPARATE purpose-scoped determinism test for the new subsystem over folding its fields into the shared fingerprint's input — the shared golden stays a stable, rarely-churning guard over the original core, while each new subsystem gets a determinism guarantee precisely shaped to what it actually needs to prove. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E4
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][narrative]` For an adaptive narrative game with a limited authoring budget, a FIXED spine of ordered beats with 2-4 director-selected variants per beat (linear content cost) can read as more responsive than a true branching tree (exponential cost, forced shallow by budget) — provided the variation is paired with legible, ATTRIBUTED callbacks to specific past choices rather than vague mood-based dressing. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E5
- `[gamedev][algorithm][gamedesign]` Delaunay triangulation + MST is the standard dungeon-layout connection algorithm, but its advantage over a simpler all-pairs-distance + MST + partial-loop-reconnection approach is scale-dependent — at small room counts (roughly a dozen or fewer) the two produce equivalent connectivity, so the simpler, fully-deterministic, no-RNG all-pairs version is the better choice: cheaper to implement, easier to verify, identical output quality. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E6
- `[gamedev][state][tooling]` Carry offline single-player progress between games as a self-contained player-pasted code (canonical-serialized + integrity checksum + signature prefix + version field), not a backend — match the mechanism to the threat model: a checksum catches typos/corruption but is NOT anti-tamper, and offline single-player has no adversary to defend against; version the format from game 1 because sequels parse old codes forever. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E12
- `[gamedev][tooling][process]` Carry state across independently-shipped saga games via a versioned+checksummed handoff CODE and no shared runtime (each game rebuilds its own spine) — buys permanent backward compat and blast-radius isolation at the price of hand-propagating every fix to each sibling that copied the spine; keep a catalog so it's a checklist, not a rediscovery. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E12
- `[process][gamedev][git]` For a saga of independently-shipped, long-lived installments, make the repos structurally independent (clone-and-reuse, never edit) and repeat the architecture/determinism spine as a documented PATTERN per repo instead of a shared engine/monorepo — trading DRY and auto-propagating fixes for the guarantee that no installment can break a shipped one; keep the unavoidable cross-installment coupling to a single explicit versioned artifact (a player-carried export code). → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E13
- `[gamedev][gamedesign]` Don't let your newest tech capability auto-become the current chapter's theme — ship the leap (real enemy AI) under a mundane in-world justification and RESERVE the on-the-nose payoff ("an intelligence gains agency") for the installment your structure assigns it; themes are a finite sequenced resource, and "we can finally build X" ≠ "this story is about X." → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-waiting-city.md#E13
- `[gamedev][state][determinism]` At the sim/presentation seam, place each feature by whether it CHANGES THE OUTCOME, not whether it feels like gameplay: outcome modifiers (difficulty, day/night damage) go in the deterministic integer core and become part of the replay/fingerprint; pure feel (shake, squash, tint) stays in presentation as event-driven render-only state — and watch the deceptive case (hit-stop "freezes the game" but is correctly a presentation-layer render/dispatch pause, not a freeze of authoritative state). → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E14
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][state]` Teach a game's verbs with a passive observer overlay that watches the real event stream and marks steps done — one canonical implementation of every verb, no tutorial mode to exit — but pin each teaching trigger to a SPECIFIC entity id (not a generic event type) or ordinary play satisfies tutorial steps by accident. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-prologue.md#E15
- `[gamedev][quest][gamedesign]` Gate discovery interactables by EFFECT not existence — spawn them always, give the not-yet-active case an explicit in-fiction no-op (a hook, not an empty map); keep only objective-CRITICAL targets existence-gated. Pick per object by what breaks on early interaction. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E8
- `[gamedev][state][gamedesign]` Keep a command-sourced sim a PURE reducer: make enemy aggression an explicit presentation-issued command and model i-frames as the driver WITHHOLDING it — no AI tick, no invuln flag in state. Core stays replayable; test timing at the seam since the fingerprint won't. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E9
- `[gamedev][process]` For a saga of independently-shipped sequels, copy stable primitives and pass continuity through a narrow versioned code rather than sharing a runtime engine — accept duplication + manual fix-porting to guarantee no sequel can break a shipped predecessor. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E10
- `[input][gamedev][ui]` If a dismiss/confirm control is shared with a high-frequency action (attack), don't dismiss on a tap — require a timed HOLD on a different button and give prompts no default selection, so combat mashing can't eat or mis-answer a story beat. Accept the friction. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E11
- `[gamedev][rendering][determinism]` A textbook algorithm you can't use as-is (binary + float shadowcasting) is not the default — a slower, purpose-built EXACT-INTEGER FOV (Bresenham + clarity-multiply over an opacity map) buys partial occlusion + determinism-safety, worth it when the working set is small. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E12
- `[gamedev][state][rendering]` A cosmetic feature that is really PROGRESSION: store the minimal authoritative unlock flag in sim state (saved, fingerprinted) and DERIVE all appearance from it in the read-only renderer — never let the renderer own progress or the look leak into sim. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-wrong-sky.md#E13
- `[gamedev][gamedesign][architecture]` A sub-progression gate WITHIN an existing construction-fixed-threshold stage (levels within a level) should derive its per-substage quota from the actual per-item tags in the content table itself, computed once at construction — not from a generic formula over aggregate counts that assumes even distribution, which silently drifts the moment the content's real distribution isn't even (which it usually won't be). → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E7
- `[gamedev][ui][cutscene]` When a cutscene/UI clock is driving BOTH something with a real correctness requirement (marker/animation timing, replay determinism) and something that's really about human reading comfort, split them into two independent clocks rather than tuning one shared duration/rate — no fixed number satisfies both a fast and a slow reader, but a player-input-driven advance pointer for the human-facing half does, by construction. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E8
- `[gamedev][ui][bug]` A word-wrap/list-row helper that returns a "next line" cursor for its caller must advance that cursor after the FINAL line too, not just on mid-loop wraps — a single-line (non-wrapping) row otherwise returns the SAME y it was drawn at, and the next stacked row silently overlaps it by one line-height; audit the no-wrap path specifically, and verify with a synthetic render at the actual reported viewport width rather than by reading the logic. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E9
- `[tooling][process][testing]` A hand-maintained sidecar mapping two files' shared structure (an Issue Form's labels ↔ their field ids) will drift silently — a field just vanishes, no error; derive the map from the one real source instead, and catch this class of bug only with an end-to-end test built from the actual upstream artifact, not a hand-typed stand-in → projects/local__collective-reference.md#E1
- `[gamedev][simulation][cellular-automaton]` A spatial reach cap (radius from origin) on a self-propagating hazard (fire/gas/infection) does NOT bound how many times a location can be re-triggered by neighbors sharing that same area — a burnt-out cell with no memory of having fired can be relit forever within the radius. Give the spreader a finite, monotonically-decreasing total-event budget (generation-capped spawn allowance) instead of/beside the radius, whenever "eventually fully extinguishes" is a real requirement. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-answering-deep.md#E11
- `[testing][state][gamedev]` A getter that exposes internal state via a shallow copy (`{...state}`) for safe external reading will silently discard any write made through it — no error, and every later read shows the unchanged original state, which reads exactly like "the game ignored my forced state" rather than a test-authoring mistake. Give tests a dedicated, explicitly-named mutator function instead of writing through a copy-returning getter. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E21
- `[testing][harness][determinism][gamedev]` A headless rAF slowdown ratio is not a stable constant across runs — never compute a fixed real-time wait from an assumed ratio, poll for the actual state transition instead. And before adopting a virtual-clock API (e.g. Playwright's `page.clock`) to make game-loop timing deterministic, verify with a cheap per-frame probe that it actually advances YOUR engine's own dt bookkeeping — it can fire rAF callbacks correctly on a trivial loop while still failing to drive a real engine's `Clock.getDelta()`-based timing. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E22
- `[gamedev][input][mobile]` A `hasTouch`/`isMobile` e2e viewport flag proves the LAYOUT survives a phone-shaped screen; it proves nothing about whether a touch-only user can press anything unless the test drives input via real touch/tap events instead of `page.keyboard` — a keyboard-driven test on a narrow viewport can pass forever while an entire device category is completely unplayable underneath it (here: a whole on-screen control layer was left "(P-later)" and never built, shipped for multiple feature passes with nobody noticing). Each supported input device needs at least one e2e test forbidden from using any OTHER device's input API. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E10
- `[gamedev][input][mobile]` An on-screen touch control placed near a screen edge with a hand-picked fixed pixel margin (`left/right/bottom: 14px`) can silently stop RECEIVING input on a real device — an OS edge-swipe-gesture band can intercept the touch before the game's listener fires, or a hardware notch/home-indicator can occlude the button — and a headless mobile-viewport e2e test (isMobile/hasTouch) will not catch this, since it emulates screen dimensions, not safe-area geometry or OS gesture interception. Inset every edge-hugging control by `env(safe-area-inset-*)` (with a 0 fallback), not a constant. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E11
- `[gamedev][accessibility][visualdesign]` Auditing a hand-curated per-entity color-ramp palette pairwise (not just "does each ramp look distinct to me") caught a real, unaddressed case: two ramps authored independently and thematically (a warm-red enemy ramp, a moss-green enemy ramp) landed on the red-green CVD confusion axis AND at nearly identical luminance — the one channel (brightness) that normally survives color-vision deficiency didn't separate them either, only their already-distinct SILHOUETTES did. Before shipping 4+ named entity ramps, check every pair's rough luminance (`0.299R+0.587G+0.114B`) and flag any pair that's ALSO on the red-green axis — that's cheap, and it's exactly where CVD confusion happens even when each ramp looked fine in isolation. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E12
- `[gamedev][web][ux]` A working, tested, backend-less share-code MECHANISM (a versioned base64 export) is exactly as unshared as no export at all if its PRESENTATION was never separately audited: no copy-to-clipboard button (canvas-drawn text can't be natively selected/copied), no Open Graph/Twitter Card tags on the page (a shared LINK previews as a bare untrusted URL), and no spoiler-free human-readable summary above the raw code (nothing for a recipient to read before importing it themselves). Audit presentation as its own explicit step once the data format works — it's a different failure mode than the format being wrong. → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E13
- `[procgen][testing]` a fixed `0..N` seed-suite validator is real completability coverage but not a growing fuzz corpus or a diversity check — both are separate, currently-absent axes → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E14
- `[input][process]` a "rebuild it fresh" phase task can silently drop an already-solved sibling-phase pattern — grep the prior phase before scaffolding a subsystem from zero → projects/pappydapimp69__the-game-the-recursion.md#E15
- `[process][git][ci]` A GitHub Actions `push` trigger scoped only by `paths:` (no `branches:` filter) fires on every branch a matching file changes on — including a contributor's own open PR branch, not just the intended integration branch. For any automation whose side effect (a bot commit, a promotion, a deploy) is meant to happen only after human review, add an explicit `branches: [main]` filter alongside `paths:`; don't rely on git commit author/email to distinguish "the bot" from "a contributor" inside a diff — that's unauthenticated and spoofable, so gate by WHEN it can run (post-merge), not WHO appears to have committed. → projects/pappydapimp69__memory.md#E2
- `[gamedesign][testing][process]` A request with multiple co-equal named requirements ("the camera should be cinematic" AND "the player performs tricks") is easy to ship half-done in a way that reads as complete: the more novel/interesting clause (camera work) gets the design attention, the tests, and an impressive demo, while a plainer clause (the tricks themselves visibly happening) goes unimplemented and unnoticed — because nothing anyone checked (state assertions, even a targeted screenshot) was ever evidence for that specific clause. After shipping, re-read the ORIGINAL request verbatim and check each named clause independently against the code, not against the demo, which naturally showcases whichever part turned out best. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E23
- `[testing][ui][gamedev]` A test-only "skip the state machine forward" hook that hand-copies only the fields the NEXT phase's logic needs (not the full side effects of the real transition it's shortcutting past — a DOM overlay hide, a camera reset) can leave the page visibly stuck in a broken/impossible state while every state-based assertion still reports PASS, because state reads never look at the DOM. Route shortcut hooks through the real transition function(s) instead of hand-duplicating a subset of their effects, and screenshot at least once specifically for hook-driven test scenarios — they're the code path least likely to already have one. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E24
- `[ui][mobile][testing]` A code comment claiming "control cluster A replaces control cluster B" is a design intent, not a guarantee — if A and B are shown/hidden by two independently-triggered toggles (different trigger events, different code paths) targeting overlapping screen space, nothing enforces they're ever actually mutually exclusive. Reconcile both conditions at one decision point (e.g. `B.hidden = deviceCheck || aIsActive`), and verify with a real touch-enabled, phone-width screenshot specifically during the state where both would-be-exclusive clusters might render — checking each cluster's own hidden-class independently can't catch the overlap, and desktop-viewport testing never exercises a mobile-only cluster at all. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E25
- `[save][state][gamedev]` A function's side effect written assuming "this only runs once" (based on having exactly one call site at the time) doesn't survive a second call site added later for an unrelated, individually-reasonable purpose — the new caller has no way to know the function secretly assumes it's the only one. Here, importing a save code mid-session (to refresh the HUD for whatever level it lands on) also re-ran a level-2 setup block that unconditionally reset an in-session boss-fight-won flag never captured in save data, silently undoing the win. Whenever adding a new call site to an existing function, audit whether that function does anything "once-only," and gate it on an existing idempotency check (e.g. `!entityAlreadySpawned`) rather than a bare unconditional reset. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E26
- `[process][documentation][gamedev]` A design/blueprint doc exported to a separate repo from its implementation (a spec repo, a wiki) has no mechanism to notice when the real implementation is later revised — the spec repo gets no failing test, no broken deploy, nothing. An "✅ IMPLEMENTED" marker is only accurate as of its own last-touched date; when a shipped feature undergoes a significant pivot (not just a bugfix), update any cross-repo spec that described the original design as part of the pivot, via a dated correction (never a silent rewrite, which destroys the "why we changed it" trail). → projects/pappydapimp69__ideas.md#E1
- `[process][documentation][taxonomy]` When the same string is a legitimate DOMAIN tag in one context and a legitimate SUB-tag (different casing/formatting rule) in another within the same free-text taxonomy, expect it to get pattern-matched into the wrong role — correct usage in the familiar role (a lowercase sub-tag) bleeds into the other role (an uppercase domain) with the wrong formatting, invisible to a normal read-through since the word itself is right. Periodically grep the taxonomy's structural positions specifically (e.g. `^## \[DOMAIN` against an established-domains list) rather than trusting a visual skim to catch a casing-only mismatch — it can make an established category's entries invisible to both search and future filing. → projects/pappydapimp69__ideas.md#E2
- `[input][gamedesign][accessibility]` A game supporting N parallel input surfaces (keyboard, touch, gamepad) for its existing actions has no structural guarantee a NEW bindable action (a minigame's input, a new ability) reaches all of them — each surface is usually its own independent listener set, and a feature can test green on two of three surfaces while the third was never even considered, with nothing failing or warning. When shipping a new player action, explicitly checklist every input surface (or route through an existing single-chokepoint action-resolver if the game has one) rather than trusting "it works" on whichever surfaces you happened to test with. → projects/pappydapimp69__dog.md#E27
- `[process][git][documentation]` An onboarding doc's absolute CI-enforcement claim ("CI rejects any contributor change outside X") can be true only for ONE triggering event (e.g. `pull_request`) while a different event touching the exact same files/branch (a plain `push`) silently skips the same check — verify an absolute onboarding claim against the actual event-scoping, not just "a workflow exists." This is most dangerous when the claim implicitly assumes another safety layer (branch protection) that has a documented history of lapsing — state the real, narrower guarantee in onboarding docs instead of the unconditional one. → projects/pappydapimp69__memory.md#E3
- `[process][github][llm]` A public LLM's fetch is an ANONYMOUS client: GitHub's contents API 403s and `/tree` is robots-blocked — only a complete raw file URL returns 200; probe the real caller's capability before building on it, design to the weakest reliable primitive, and suspect one unverified primitive when many surface "can't read" bugs cluster. → projects/pappydapimp69__collective.md#E1
- `[process][agent][design]` When scoping an autonomous mode, gate on the property that truly makes a task decidable (objective vs human-only signal), not a proxy category like "fact-lookup only" — the proxy both under-powered it (banned artifact-settled "fit" work) and misplaced the real line (only human-reaction gaps are actually off-limits). → projects/pappydapimp69__collective.md#E2
- `[security][github][ci]` Interpolating `${{ github.event.issue.body }}` into an Actions `run:` heredoc is shell injection — `${{ }}` expands into the script TEXT before the shell runs; pass webhook-supplied fields via `env:` and read them with `printf %s` so they stay inert data. → projects/pappydapimp69__collective.md#E3
- `[process][github][agent]` Opening an issue/PR is a request to persist, not persistence — never report "saved" from an intermediate step; use explicit states (drafted/submitted/pending-review/recorded) and verify the landed artifact (a re-checked fingerprint on main) before claiming recorded. → projects/pappydapimp69__collective.md#E4
- `[security][prompt-engineering][process]` A user-pasted prompt is attacker-editable UX, not a control surface — enforce every load-bearing rule (allowed modes, scope, never-close, path sandbox) in server-side code that runs regardless of the prompt and default-deny there; accept that rules then live in both places and can drift. → projects/pappydapimp69__collective.md#E5
- `[process][harness][git]` A well-reasoned judgment call to override an automated review gate on shared canon still isn't authorization to act on it — a terse instruction ("run steward") doesn't name a specific override decision; surface the fork before touching anything hard to reverse, especially a deletion → projects/pappydapimp69__memory.md#E4
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` cellular-automata cave smoothing stabilizes fast but does NOT guarantee connectivity — density 0.40-0.50 is both the best-looking AND the most connectivity-fragile range → projects/local__sandbox-procgen-ca.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` an L-system's bounding box grows ~9x/iteration while segment count grows ~5x — cap iteration count, don't use it as a detail dial → projects/local__sandbox-procgen-lsystem.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` eager fixpoint propagation prevented WFC contradictions across 3 adversarial tilesets and 14x14 grids — verify propagation completeness before adding backtracking → projects/local__sandbox-procgen-wfc.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` weighted A*'s speed/quality tradeoff is steeply nonlinear — nearly all the useful speedup lives at weight~1.5-2, further weight mostly just degrades path quality → projects/local__sandbox-pathfinding-astar-heuristic.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` unconstrained 8-directional pathfinding corner-cuts through wall gaps in 100% of solvable maps at moderate wall density — treat prevention as required correctness, not polish → projects/local__sandbox-pathfinding-diagonal.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev]` the flow-field-vs-per-agent-A* crossover lands at ~5-7 simultaneous agents regardless of map size (15x15 to 40x40 tested), since both costs scale together → projects/local__sandbox-pathfinding-flowfield.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state][gamedesign]` Multiplicative decay applied uniformly across a weighted pool (e.g. loot-table duplicate protection) converges given enough iterations — to the ORIGINAL ratios if floored (every item hits the same floor), or to UNIFORM selection if unfloored (every item underflows to exact zero). A hard exclusion/cap (discrete eligible/ineligible) has no asymptote to decay into; verify any decay-based fairness mechanic with a long-run simulation, since short-term behavior looks correct right up until it inverts. → projects/local__sandbox-loot-pity.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state]` A "fallback for float-rounding safety" branch that returns a plausible default on ANY unexpected input — not just the narrow rounding case it was written for — silently absorbs a much more serious failure (e.g. total weight collapsing to zero) behind the same non-crashing, normal-looking return value. Scope the fallback to its exact intended condition and make every other unexpected case (zero/negative total) fail loudly instead of sharing its catch-all path. → projects/local__sandbox-loot-pity.md#E2
- `[algorithm][testing]` A*'s shortest-path guarantee depends entirely on heuristic admissibility, and a non-admissible heuristic fails SILENTLY — a poisoned/overestimating heuristic value produces a normal-looking, wrong (non-optimal) path with the same success return shape as a correct one, no crash or warning. Any heuristic beyond a textbook-proven-admissible function needs its admissibility tested directly and independently — never infer path-optimality from "the pathfinder ran without error." → projects/local__sandbox-astar-replan.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state][gamedev]` A replanner that re-derives its start position from "wherever the agent currently is" must treat that position as unconditional ground truth — reusing a static pathfinder's "reject blocked start" guard rejects legitimate replans the moment an obstacle spawns at/under an already-positioned agent (unit stacking, moving hazards, closing doors), freezing it even when a route exists one step away. Occupancy data gates where you can GO, never whether you're allowed to already BE somewhere. → projects/local__sandbox-astar-replan.md#E2
- `[algorithm][state]` A version dispatcher gated on one relational comparison (`version < LATEST`) silently treats "missing version" and "newer-than-known version" the same as "already migrated" — both fail the `<` check and fall through with zero warning, passing unmigrated or corrupted data downstream untouched. Explicitly branch invalid / newer-than-known / at-latest as three distinct cases before the migrate loop, not one comparison operator. → projects/local__sandbox-savemigrate.md#E1
- `[algorithm][state][testing]` A schema-migration step that RENAMES/ADDS fields is idempotent for free; one that RESHAPES structure (flat→nested, string→object) is not — reprocessing its own output looks like fresh valid input and silently double-wraps data with no error. Guard reshape steps on the item's actual current shape, and test every structural migrator by calling it twice in a row before trusting it in any pipeline that could retry or resume. → projects/local__sandbox-savemigrate.md#E2
- `[state][testing]` A migrated field can look fully present in memory (`'y' in obj` → true) while `JSON.stringify` silently drops it because its value is `undefined` — validating a migration/transform by inspecting the in-memory object alone misses this; always round-trip through the ACTUAL persistence format before asserting correctness, since undefined-vs-absent-key only diverges at the serialization boundary. → projects/local__sandbox-savemigrate.md#E3
- `[algo][testing][process]` The standard Bloom filter false-positive formula `(1-e^(-kn/m))^k` is a proven LOWER bound (Jensen's inequality), not a two-sided estimate — measured 4x the predicted rate here even with a bit-fill fraction matching theory exactly; never trust the formula's output as a design guarantee, measure the real rate → projects/local__sandbox-bloom.md#E1
- `[parsing][text-processing][testing]` A "split on newline, then split on delimiter" parser doesn't just mis-parse a field when a quoted value contains the delimiter — a quoted NEWLINE silently splits one row into two, corrupting the row count itself, which is more dangerous than a wrong field because it doesn't look wrong → projects/local__sandbox-csv.md#E1
- `[algo][state][determinism]` Python's built-in `hash()` is randomized per-process (PYTHONHASHSEED) — a Bloom filter (or any hash-based structure) built from it gives DIFFERENT bit positions for the same item across process restarts, silently breaking any filter meant to be persisted or shared; verified by hashing the identical input in two separate process runs and observing different results → projects/local__sandbox-bloom.md#E2
- `[algo][state][process]` A token-bucket (or any `now - last_seen` delta) must clamp elapsed time to non-negative — a backward clock read (NTP correction, out-of-order callers) otherwise silently DRAINS tokens from a request that cost nothing → projects/local__sandbox-ratelimit.md#E1
- `[ai][gamedev][state]` Multiplicative-veto utility AI only guarantees a zero-scoring consideration vetoes an action — a flat additive hysteresis bonus can still let a merely-high competitor permanently outrank a maximal (1.0) urgent one when their ranges overlap; fix with a saturation-based urgent-override bypass → projects/local__sandbox-combined-integration.md#E1
- `[algorithm][gamedev][state]` A pathfinder proving a path exists doesn't help if the caller derives movement from a separate greedy heuristic instead of consuming the actual returned path — invisible to unit tests of the pathfinder itself → projects/local__sandbox-combined-integration.md#E2
- `[ai][gamedev][algorithm]` Three successive flee-AI decision-layer fixes (sticky sub-goal, urgent override) each moved their own target metric but left total caught-time byte-for-byte unchanged — with equal per-tick movement budgets a reactive pursuer relocks onto an equal-speed evader regardless of goal-selection quality; check movement-speed parity before tuning decision logic → projects/local__sandbox-combined-integration.md#E3
- `[gamedev][input][determinism]` In a frame-stepped/tick system where one step reads input and another advances state, a flag that GATES the input read must be updated before that read within the same tick — set it after (e.g. in the phase-advance branch) and the window opens one frame late by construction. In action combat this is cancels/links that "feel dropped" on tight timing; it hides behind a cancel-ASAP/re-sample policy on loose input, so test with frame-perfect scripted sequences. → projects/local__sandbox-dbz-combat.md#E1
- `[gamedesign][balance]` A menu of combat options (skills/moves/tiers/upgrades) that differ only along a monotonic power axis (raw damage, a flat multiplier) collapses to ONE dominant choice — and per cost the biggest-number option is often the worst — which is the mechanical root of "the abilities feel unimpressive." Give each option Pareto structure: lead exactly one orthogonal axis (target shape, burst-vs-sustain, range, efficiency, startup role, knockback-utility) and pay on another; audit by checking each option is ever uniquely optimal across real encounter types. → projects/local__sandbox-dbz-combat.md#E2
- `[testing][state]` When aggregating per-case "best option" results into a coverage/diversity metric, guard the no-winner sentinel (`null`/`None`/`-1`/`''`) before folding it in — an unhandled sentinel silently pollutes the tally (often coerced into a phantom set member) and flips a "nothing could do this" case into apparent coverage. Surface no-result cases as their own category; they're usually the key finding, not noise to swallow. → projects/local__sandbox-dbz-combat.md#E3
- `[gamedesign][balance]` A combat resource (ki/mana/cooldown/sustain-drain) shapes decisions only while its cost BINDS within the decision horizon — too cheap makes the powered option always-on and dominant, too dear makes it unwinnable. Keep it persistent (shared across the horizon, not per-fight refilled), price a spend to beat your default DPS by enough to cover its downtime, scale costs WITH the pool so it keeps binding across the level curve (flat costs against a scaling pool is why "the endgame is just spam the biggest move"), and cap effects whose value never falls off with scale. → projects/local__sandbox-dbz-combat.md#E4
- `[algorithm][graph][state]` Any traversal that assumes a DAG (recursive dataflow eval, longest-path layer ranking, dependency resolution) overflows or never terminates on a cycle — establish acyclicity as a PRECONDITION, don't discover the cycle when it blows up. Reject the cycle-creating edge at connect-time if you own the graph (DFS: is `from` reachable from `to`?), or reverse a feedback-arc set if you only render it; filter self-loops out as decoration, since reversing a self-edge is a no-op that still diverges. → projects/local__sandbox-graph-viz.md#E1
- `[state][graph]` In a graph / entity-with-relationships store, deleting a node without removing its incident edges leaves dangling references that crash a later, unrelated-looking consumer (`TypeError` when the evaluator dereferences the missing node). A delete must cascade to every incident edge atomically — fix referential integrity at the mutation, not with defensive null-checks at every read site (keep those only as defense-in-depth for externally-loaded data). → projects/local__sandbox-graph-viz.md#E2
- `[algorithm][graph][state]` Three edge cases break a textbook force-directed layout, each with a standard fix a connected-graph happy path never surfaces: coincident nodes give d=0 → repulsion/d²=Inf → Inf×0=NaN poisoning every position (floor the distance with a soft-core min); disconnected components drift unbounded because nothing balances inter-component repulsion (add a weak centering/gravity force — structural, not fixable by more iterations); and integration stability is set by timestep × the stiffest gradient (springs) plus damping, NOT by the loud self-limiting repulsion. → projects/local__sandbox-graph-viz.md#E3
- `[algorithm][determinism][testing]` The terminal state of an iterative solver/optimizer is not its answer. Gate the stop condition on the TRUE residual/objective (finite-guarded, so a NaN can't read as "settled" and a cooling schedule shrinking the step can't fake convergence), and when the process isn't monotone keep and return the best-by-objective iterate, not the last one (detect period-2 to stop). A per-step-displacement proxy shrinks under both annealing and divergence — reporting "done" exactly when it shouldn't. → projects/local__sandbox-graph-viz.md#E4
- `[git][tooling]` Summing per-commit positive deltas across a file's git history to build a "growth over time" event stream does NOT reliably sum to the file's true current total — a rename, rebase, or hand-corrected anchor can inflate the running total without ever producing a negative delta to offset it (drops are silently ignored, not retracted). Compute the true total independently (parse HEAD once) and clamp the derived event stream to it, rather than trusting delta-sum arithmetic across a history that isn't guaranteed to be purely additive. → projects/pappydapimp69__brain.md#E2
- `[tooling][parsing]` A block-accumulation parser (group continuation lines, search the whole block once it ends) must route EVERY condition that can end a block — a new record starting, a blank line, EOF — through the same search-then-clear function; searching only on the "obvious" end condition (e.g. the next bullet) and silently discarding on the others drops any block whose end was triggered a different way. Cross-check the parser's total against an independent ground-truth count (e.g. a raw `grep -c`) — this class of bug produces no error, just a silently smaller number. → projects/pappydapimp69__brain.md#E3
